# Hang's technical vocabulary

This list collects technical terms and estimates how familiar each one is to Hang. The estimate comes from how often, and in how many studies, Hang's papers use a term. 15 dissertations are a second source. Three of them, by Short, Vázquez, and Bobu, are the committee's style that Hang named. The other 12 are recent dissertations from the labs of Matarić, Song, Li, Vázquez, and Scassellati, and show the vocabulary that current readers in robot learning and human-robot interaction know. The dissertations are counted in their own column and never raise a tier. The list covers 1100 terms in 15 groups, 69 acronyms, and 43 terms that Hang has met only in the titles of cited work. 374 of the terms are Reference only. [word-list.md](word-list.md) lists every word the papers use, with the same familiarity estimate, and the words only the reference dissertations use.

Use it together with [Hang's writing style reference](hangs-writing-style-reference.md). When drafting or revising dissertation prose, prefer terms marked Core or Familiar. A term marked Once, Reference only, or Cited only is one Hang seldom or never writes, so check its sense or define it. A word that is in none of the lists falls outside the vocabulary. `python writing_style_ref/tools/check_vocabulary.py <file>` lists such words in a draft.

Built on November 8, 2026 from 53,764 words of Hang's own text in 25 files and 551,838 words of the 15 reference dissertations (147,025 in the committee group and 404,813 in the lab group).

## How familiarity is estimated

| Tier | Rule | What it suggests |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Core | Used in at least three of the six studies and at least five times, or in two studies at least twenty times. | Hang writes it routinely. Use it without explanation. |
| Familiar | Used in two studies, or at least five times in one. | Hang has written it in more than a passing way. Use it freely. |
| Dissertation term | Not in the papers, but the writing style reference sets it as the dissertation's wording. | Hang chose it for the dissertation. Use it as the style reference entry says. |
| Used | Used two to four times, all in one study. | Hang has written it, in one context. Keep to that sense. |
| Once | Used once. | Hang has written it once. Prefer a Core or Familiar word, or check the sense. |
| Reference only | Not in the papers, but used in a reference dissertation: Short, Vázquez, or Bobu, or one of the 12 recent lab dissertations. | The field uses it, but Hang has not written it. Define it on first use, and check that the field meaning fits. |
| Cited only | Not in the papers or the reference dissertations, but in the title of a cited work. | Hang has seen it in a title. Gloss it on first use, or ask before relying on it. |

Terms per tier: 290 Core, 296 Familiar, 7 Dissertation term, 88 Used, 39 Once, 374 Reference only, 3 Cited only, 3 Not found.

A use is one occurrence in a paper or a draft. A study is one of the six paper families below. The count is by word form, so a common word such as state, return, or progress is counted in every sense, and a term also counts inside a longer term (reward function inside learned reward function). Drafts count as Hang's own writing, since they are earlier versions written for the same studies. Each paper was coauthored, and CHARM was written with a co-first author, so a term found only in CHARM is weaker evidence of Hang's own word choice. The prospectus is single-authored. Comments, math, citations, and the notes of coauthors and advisors in the LaTeX are not counted.

The Reference dissertations column counts the same term in the reference dissertations. Each committee dissertation is named with its count. The lab dissertations are summed, with the number of the 12 that use the term and the one that uses it most, so "lab 212 in 9/12 (Tsoi 80)" means 212 uses in nine lab dissertations, 80 of them by Tsoi, and "lab: Klein 30" means that only Klein uses it. A high count there and none in the papers means the term belongs to the field, or to a neighboring field, more than to Hang's own writing. A term used by many lab dissertations is common in the field today; one used heavily by a single dissertation may be that writer's topic. Where the papers have no example, the example comes from a reference dissertation and is marked *Reference*. Those examples quote another writer and are not models of Hang's wording.

Only the abstract and the chapters are used. The front matter, the tables of contents, the appendices, and the bibliography are left out, and running heads and page numbers are removed. The text was extracted with pdftotext (`tools/extract_reference_text.py`). The committee text is in `sources/reference-dissertations/`. The lab dissertations are not bundled with the plugin: their PDFs and text are in the repository's `dissertation_reference/` folder, whose README says how they were chosen and which requested dissertations could not be retrieved. Candon and Thompson are author-posted drafts, and Sargent's PDF has no abstract text. Bobu's equations come out of the PDF as unreadable symbols, so a few counts of short words there may be off.

## Field terms in none of the sources

These 3 standard terms were searched for and are in neither the papers, the reference dissertations, nor the cited titles. Hang may still know them, but the sources give no evidence of it. Where a near term is listed above, it is named.

| Term | Group | Nearest term the papers use |
| --- | --- | --- |
| practice session | Study design and data collection | practice, as in minutes to practice |
| demonstration interface | Study design and data collection | control interface |
| annotation interface | Study design and data collection | graphical user interface |

## Common academic words and the papers

These 79 words and phrases are frequent in academic prose and in text drafted by assistants. Each was searched for in the papers and in the reference dissertations.

**Used in the papers** (15). thus (52), leverage (33), moreover (23), utilize (12), furthermore (11), on the other hand (10), in contrast (5), consolidate (4), intricate (2), thereby (2), facilitate (2), streamline (1), noteworthy (1), trajectory-level (1), that said (1).

**Not used in the papers** (64). albeit (Bobu 2 · lab: Tsoi 2), articulate (lab 24 in 7/12 (Gokmen 10)), as such (Bobu 20 · lab 17 in 7/12 (Georgiou 6)), bolster (lab: Matheus 3), by contrast (Bobu 4 · lab 13 in 4/12 (Thompson 6)), complement (Short 1 · Vázquez 3 · lab 21 in 5/12 (Ghose 10)), comprehensive (Bobu 2 · lab 35 in 10/12 (Gokmen 12)), corroborate (lab: Sargent 1), crucially (Bobu 3 · lab 9 in 4/12 (Wong 3)), crux, culminate (Short 1), delineate, delve (lab 4 in 2/12 (Matheus 3)), dichotomy, discern (Bobu 1 · lab: Matheus 3), elicit (Short 2 · Vázquez 1 · Bobu 1 · lab 25 in 6/12 (Birmingham 6)), elucidate (lab: Georgiou 1), exemplify (Bobu 2 · lab: Shi 1), facet (lab 9 in 3/12 (Shi 7)), first-class (lab 2 in 2/12 (Thompson 1)), foreground, foster (Short 5 · lab 23 in 4/12 (Matheus 13)), granular (lab 8 in 4/12 (Tsoi 4)), harness (Short 1 · lab 5 in 4/12 (Tsoi 2)), hence (Bobu 14 · lab 16 in 6/12 (Thompson 5)), holistic (lab 11 in 4/12 (Klein 6)), illuminate (Vázquez 1 · Bobu 1 · lab 8 in 3/12 (Matheus 6)), in particular (Short 1 · Vázquez 21 · Bobu 3 · lab 59 in 9/12 (Tsoi 12)), in turn (Short 1 · Vázquez 2 · Bobu 4 · lab 19 in 8/12 (Klein 7)), instantiate (Short 24 · Bobu 6 · lab 19 in 6/12 (Wong 9)), interplay (lab 13 in 6/12 (Candon 4)), interrogate (lab: Tsoi 1), intriguing, landscape (lab 7 in 2/12 (Matheus 6)), mediate (Short 3 · lab 13 in 2/12 (Birmingham 8)), meticulous, multifaceted (lab 5 in 4/12 (Klein 2)), myriad (lab 2 in 2/12 (Sargent 1)), necessitate (Bobu 2 · lab 11 in 5/12 (Matheus 6)), notably (Short 1 · Bobu 1 · lab 44 in 10/12 (Matheus 22)), nuanced (Bobu 1 · lab 11 in 6/12 (Matheus 4)), operationalize (Bobu 5 · lab 4 in 2/12 (Tsoi 3)), overarching (lab 7 in 5/12 (Tsoi 2)), paramount, pivotal (Short 1 · lab 5 in 4/12 12)), substantiate (lab: Matheus 1), synergy (lab: Candon 1), taken together (lab 12 in 7/12 (Ghose 4)), to this end (Vázquez 1 · lab 18 in 4/12 (Tsoi 9)), underpin (lab 3 in 2/12 (Thompson 2)), A word in the second list is not wrong, but Hang has not used it in the papers. Counts in parentheses show where a reference dissertation uses it. Hang's own wording takes priority over the reference dissertations, as in the writing style reference, so in prose written for Hang choose a word from the lists above unless Hang introduces it.

## Rebuilding

Edit `tools/vocab_authored.py` to add, move, or remove a term, then run `python writing_style_ref/tools/build_vocabulary.py` from the dissertation folder. Set `VOCAB_PAPERS` to the dissertation's `reference_papers/` when the papers are not next to this folder, and `VOCAB_LAB_DISSERTATIONS` to the folder of the lab dissertations when it is not the repository's `dissertation_reference/`. The build prints terms that it could not find and frequent words from the papers that no group lists yet. To check a draft against the vocabulary, run `python writing_style_ref/tools/check_vocabulary.py chapters/<file>.tex`. General English frequencies come from a snapshot of wordfreq in `sources/general-english-zipf.tsv`, made with `tools/make_general_english.py`. The reference dissertation text is extracted again with `tools/extract_reference_text.py`.
